Use when the user runs /design-sync or says \"sync my design system to Claude Design\".","argumentHint":"[<project hint, e.g. \"Acme DS\">]","builtin":true},{"name":"dataviz","description":"Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"update-config","description":"Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"verify","description":"Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"debug","description":"Enable debug logging for this session and help diagnose issues","argumentHint":"[issue description]","builtin":true},{"name":"code-review","description":"Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs (plus reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups where the model's review recipe covers them) at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.","argumentHint":"[low|medium|high|xhigh|max|ultra] [--fix] [--comment] [<pr#>|<branch>|<path>]","aliases":["review"],"builtin":true},{"name":"simplify","description":"Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.","argumentHint":"[<target>]","builtin":true},{"name":"batch","description":"Research and plan a large-scale change, then execute it in parallel across 5–30 isolated worktree agents that each open a PR.","argumentHint":"<instruction>","builtin":true},{"name":"fewer-permission-prompts","description":"Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"doctor","description":"Health-check the user's Claude Code setup and fix issues: diagnose installation health — what the `claude doctor` terminal diagnostics cover — from local data (duplicate or leftover installs, PATH, unparseable settings files, broken or colliding agent definitions, skills whose frontmatter fails to parse); find unused skills, MCP servers, and plugins versus their context cost and disable dead weight; deduplicate local CLAUDE.md files against checked-in ones; trim checked-in CLAUDE.md files by cutting content a session could derive from the codebase (directory layouts, tech-stack lists, architecture overviews) while keeping gotchas, rationale, and non-standard conventions; migrate always-loaded CLAUDE.md guidance into lazy skills and nested CLAUDE.md files; flag slow hooks and context-heavy extensions; check the installed version is current; make auto mode the default permission mode; and pre-approve frequently denied read-only commands. Use when the user asks for a doctor run, checkup, audit, tune-up, or cleanup of their Claude Code setup or configuration.","argumentHint":"","aliases":["checkup"],"builtin":true},{"name":"loop","description":"Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). and mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser tool instead. It covers the pane's persistent sign-ins, tabs and preview_start, reading pages as text, site approvals, what the pane cannot open, and what to do when it cannot be reached. It is not for Claude in Chrome (mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["built-in-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:chrome-browser","description":"Read this skill before the first step that uses Claude in Chrome, the browser extension whose tools are named mcp__claude-in-chrome__* (also called Chrome, the browser extension, or the external browser) and which acts in the person's real Chrome with their own sign-ins; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__claude-in-chrome tool instead. It covers loading the tools in one ToolSearch call, checking the person's open tabs and working in a new tab, site permissions, GIF recordings, console logs, dialogs to avoid, and when to stop and ask. It is not for the built-in browser (mcp__Claude_Browser__* or mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["chrome-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:computer-use","description":"Read this skill before the first step of any request to do something in an app on the person's own computer (Notes, Finder, System Settings, any desktop app), to look at their screen, or for \"computer use\". Computer use (desktop control) lets Claude take screenshots of the person's desktop and control it with clicks, typing and scrolling through the Claude desktop app; its tools are named mcp__computer-use__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__computer_* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before computer use is turned on for a conversation there may be no such tools, only an enable__mcp__remote-devices__computer tool, which turns it on. It covers turning it on, picking the right tool, the access flow, and the safety rules for tiered apps, links and financial actions. It is not for websites, which go through Claude in Chrome or the built-in browser and their own skills. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["computer-use"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:deep-research","description":"Use this skill when the user's prompt requires (1) researching a topic across multiple sources, comparing options or alternatives, analyzing trends or history, understanding markets or industries, or reviewing literature or studies and (2) synthesizing that research into a comprehensive, narrative report. If you're planning to search the web or internal knowledge bases, consider using this skill. This skill coordinates research subagents, so use it only when you have a tool for spawning subagents (the Agent or Task tool); otherwise, research the question directly. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":""},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docs","description":"docs (living docs people share, comment on and edit; use only when the user asks for one: names a doc, document, page, memo, spec, PRD, runbook or write-up, asks for somewhere to share or keep editing something, or says yes to your doc offer; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat (at most a one-line doc offer); a report, status update, recap or \"something I can send them\" with no form named → ask first: reply, doc or file?; tabs hold tables and live charts too; a pasted claude.ai/code/artifact/… link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; not HTML pages, apps or plain chat answers; a .docx/.pptx/.xlsx/PDF asked for by name → that format's skill): asked for one → no docs-connector instructions in context? call the docs connector's `guide` with topic.instructions first, then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached. Documenting code means docstrings or repo docs, not a doc. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docs"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docx","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:import-memory","description":"Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["import-memory"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:morning","description":"Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["morning"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pdf","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pdf"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pptx","description":"Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pptx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:setup-writing-style","description":"Learns how the user writes from their own sent messages and docs, and builds a voice profile so future drafts sound like them instead of generic AI. The profile is saved as the my-writing-style skill. Use when the user asks to set up, learn, or capture their writing voice, or complains that drafts sound generic or unlike them and no my-writing-style profile exists. Only for drafting text the user will send as themselves, not for Claude's own replies. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["setup-writing-style"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:skill-creator","description":"Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["skill-creator"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:xlsx","description":"Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["xlsx"]}],"uuid":"4e2af7ff-3e3c-4d90-aac9-ebc482614c56","session_id":"36148bec-0d82-47e8-b0dd-b970b025de87"}

The\n245\t            # remint budget mirrors DEFAULT_MAX_REQUEUES: after two\n246\t            # successors the item is disclosed exhausted, not re-minted.\n247\t            from .agent_invocations import derive_request_state\n248\t            from .agent_surface import REMINT_ELIGIBLE_DEAD_STATES\n249\t\n250\t            existing_id = str(existing_request.get(\"request_id\"))\n251\t            state = derive_request_state(request_id=existing_id, base_dir=base_dir)\n252\t            if state not in REMINT_ELIGIBLE_DEAD_STATES:\n253\t                mark_consumed(base_dir, queue_item_id=qid, The generic queue projection is the\n        # default; a mission whose `next_action` is a kernel-owned pointer\n        # (`mission_dispatch.NEXT_ACTION_CONTRACTS`) mints ITS contract\n        # instead — a self_improvement mission asks for evidence_paths /\n        # problem / proposed_change so the accepted answer can reach\n        # `propose_self_change`. Before this, the pointer travelled as free\n        # prompt text and no kernel path ever read it (confirmed live\n        # 2026-09-12; the ORPHAN-694 class).\n        must_satisfy: list[dict[str, Any]] = [{\n            \"id\": \"queue_item_projected\",\n            \"description\": \"Resolve the queued next-cycle item or produce a concrete blocked reason.\",\n            \"required\": True,\n        }]\n        allowed_scope: list[str] = [\"aria-kernel/**\", \"aria-tools/**\", \".claude/**\"]\n        if pressure_id.startswith(\"mission:\"):\n            # A mission-selection item: the queue key is the mission marker,\n            # and the mission row itself carries the evidence refs its work\n            # accumulated. Resolving here keeps the drain's rule uniform —\n            # refs come from the SOURCE record, never from the identifier.\n            from .mission import fold_mission\n            from .mission_dispatch import contract_for_mission, in_flight_mission_request\n\n            mission_id = pressure_id.split(\":\", 1)[1]\n            try:\n                mission_row = fold_mission(mission_id=mission_id, base_dir=base_dir)\n            except GovernanceError:\n                mission_row = None\n            if mission_row:\n                evidence_refs = [\n                    str(ref) for ref in (mission_row.get(\"evidence_refs\") or [])\n                    if isinstance(ref, str) and ref\n                ]\n                try:\n                    contract = contract_for_mission(\n                        mission_row=mission_row, queue_item_id=qid, source_cycle_id=source_cycle or None,\n                    )\n                except GovernanceError as exc:\n                    append_tools_governance(\n                        base_dir,\n                        \"next_cycle_queue_projection_failed\",\n                        {\"queue_item_id\": qid, \"error\": str(exc)},\n                    )\n                    continue\n                if contract is not None:\n                    # A kernel contract is asked once per MISSION, not once\n                    # per queue item: the scheduler re-selects a DISCOVERED\n                    # mission every cycle (nothing moves it until the answer\n                    # lands), and `append_pending` de-duplicates only among\n                    # pending rows, so the second cycle's item is a routine\n                    # duplicate of a question still in flight. The item is\n                    # consumed against the standing request — its answer is\n                    # the answer this item was queued for.\n                    standing = in_flight_mission_request(\n                        mission_id=mission_id, contract=contract, base_dir=base_dir,\n                    )\n                    if standing is not None:\n                        mark_consumed(base_dir, queue_item_id=qid, consumed_by=daemon_agent_id)\n                        append_tools_governance(\n                            base_dir,\n                            \"next_cycle_queue_item_mission_request_in_flight\",\n                            {\n                                \"queue_item_id\": qid,\n                                \"mission_id\": mission_id,\n                                \"request_id\": standing.get(\"request_id\"),\n                                \"request_state\": standing.get(\"request_state\"),\n                                \"contract_ids\": sorted(contract.contract_ids),\n                            },\n                        )\n                        consumed += 1\n                        continue\n                    prompt = contract.prompt\n                    must_satisfy = contract.must_satisfy\n                    allowed_scope = contract.allowed_scope\n        elif source_cycle and pressure_id:\n            try:\n                pressure_record = explain_pressure(\n                    cycle_id=source_cycle, pressure_id=pressure_id, base_dir=base_dir,\n                )\n                evidence_refs = [\n                    str(path) for path in pressure_record.get(\"evidence\") or []\n                    if isinstance(path, str) and path\n                ]\n            except (ValueError, OSError):\n                # No stored pressure payload for that cycle — fall through to\n                # the queue-item marker so the mint still traces to something.\n                evidence_refs = []\n        if not evidence_refs:\n            evidence_refs = [str(qid)]\n        try:\n            request = create_agent_invocation_request(\n                target_agent=\"aria-autonomy-planner\",\n                role=\"maintenance_utility\",\n                suggested_prompt=json.dumps(prompt, indent=2, sort_keys=True),\n                must_satisfy=must_satisfy,\n                allowed_scope=allowed_scope,\n                target_sha=target_sha,\n                evidence_refs=evidence_refs,\n                pressure_event_id=pressure_id or None,\n                remint_of=remint_of,\n                base_dir=base_dir,\n            )\n        except Exception as exc:\n            append_tools_governance(\n                base_dir,\n                \"next_cycle_queue_projection_failed\",\n                {\"queue_item_id\": qid, \"error\": str(exc)},\n            )\n            continue\n        mark_consumed(base_dir, queue_item_id=qid, consumed_by=daemon_agent_id)\n        append_tools_governance(\n            base_dir,\n            \"next_cycle_queue_item_projected\",\n            {\"queue_item_id\": qid, \"request_id\": request.get(\"request_id\")},\n        )\n        consumed += 1\n    return consumed","numLines":220,"startLine":200,"totalLines":2833}}}

Y7 follow-through (ORPHAN-708) — the kernel's OWN adjudication mint\n    # (human_required_adjudication.open_adjudication) issues\n    # ``human-required:<request-id>`` refs, and the first real panel drain\n    # showed this validator rejecting them: mint-side and law-side of the\n    # same kernel disagreed, every panel opinion died submit_rejected, and\n    # the rejection BURNED the envelope's requeue budget. The ref is a\n    # LEDGER POINTER, not a repo path — the load-bearing verification\n    # happens at fold time (fold_adjudication / adjudicate_human_required\n    # resolve the record from the store before any disposition acts), so\n    # the law admits the pointer as checked rather than pretending a repo\n    # check that cannot exist for state-store records.\n    if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(ref):\n        checked.append(ref)\n        return\n    if allow_kernel_artifacts:\n        admissible, reason = _kernel_artifact_verdict(ref, root)\n        if admissible:\n            checked.append(ref)\n            return\n        if reason != \"artifact_not_recorded_in_results_ledger\":\n            # It IS an artifact path — just not one the ledger vouches for.\n            # Naming the failure is the point: a silent fall-through to the\n            # repo check would report \"not repo verified\" about a file that\n            # was never meant to be in the repo.\n            errors.append({\n                \"code\": \"agent_evidence_artifact_unverifiable\",\n                \"ref\": ref,\n                \"reason\": reason,\n            })\n            return\n    parsed = _parse_agent_ref(ref)\n    if parsed is None:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_ref_malformed\", \"ref\": ref})\n        return\n    path, line = parsed\n    # Plan 024 v3 §H-5 — canonical-resolve BEFORE the SELF_OUTPUT\n    # prefix check. Pre-fix the prefix match operated on the\n    # lexically-normalized string; a `src/../aria-tools/...` traversal\n    # could bypass detection if normalize_path didn't fully collapse\n    # it. Post-fix the resolution runs first, then SELF_OUTPUT is\n    # decided on the canonical posix-relative form.\n    from .tool_registry import GovernanceError as _GE\n    try:\n        rel_str, absolute = _canonical_evidence_path(path, root)\n    except _GE as exc:\n        msg = str(exc)\n        if msg.startswith(\"evidence_path_outside_repo\"):\n            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_escapes_workspace\", \"path\": path})\n        else:\n            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_unresolvable\", \"path\": path,\n                           \"error\": msg})\n        return\n    # ARIA self-output / runtime artefacts are NOT admissible evidence\n    # for an agent claim (CLAUDE.md L1, Plan 016 §Agent output untrusted).\n    if not allow_self_output and rel_str.startswith(SELF_OUTPUT_MARKERS):\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_self_output\", \"path\": rel_str})\n        return\n    if not absolute.exists() or not absolute.is_file():\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_missing\", \"path\": path})\n        return\n    checked.append(rel_str)\n    if line is None:\n        return\n    if line <= 0:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_invalid\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line})\n        return\n    line_count = len(absolute.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\").splitlines())\n    if line > line_count:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_missing\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line, \"line_count\": line_count})\n\n\ndef validate_agent_response_evidence(\n    *,\n    response: dict[str, Any],\n    workspace_root: str | Path,\n    request: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n    probe_session: GitProbeSession | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Re-fetch every evidence ref claimed by an agent response and verify it exists in the repo.\n\n    Why: Plan 016 V-24 — agent output is data, not truth. Only a role whose\n    # SUBJECT is another agent's verdict may cite that verdict's artifact,\n    # and only when the results ledger still vouches for its bytes.\n    allow_kernel_artifacts = str(\n        (request or {}).get(\"role\") or \"\",\n    ) in ARBITRATION_ROLES\n    # ONE probe session for the whole submission (evidence_probe): the\n    # baseline is resolved once for all refs, and every git probe of this\n    # decision shares one liveness clock — the seam that used to hand each\n    # ref a 5 s budget and re-dispatched the whole paid run on one stall.\n    # The submit decision passes the session it already resolved its\n    # evidence target on, so a target git could not read is remembered here\n    # and every ref grades `verification_unavailable` rather than being","numLines":140,"startLine":440,"totalLines":774}}}

(\"evidence_error\", \"scope_violation\") or run.get(\"evidence_validation\", {}).get(\"repository_mutation_attempt\"):\n            pressures.append(\n                _pressure(\n tool_id=str(run.get(\"tool_id\")),\n                ),\n            )\n        delta = _raw_finding_delta(run, cycle_id, root)\n        if run.get(\"status\") == \"ok\" and delta > 0:\n recommended_action=\"sample and judge increased SHADOW findings before calibration\",\n                    tool_id=str(run.get(\"tool_id\")),\n                ),\n            )\n\n    pressures.extend(_uncertainty_repeat_pressures(root, weights=_weights, cycle_id=cycle_id))\n    _apply_drift_class_weights(pressures, drift_class_weights)\n    _filter_candidate_tools(root, Weighted at\n35\t    # the top of the table: it is confirmed (CI ran the code), it is OURS\n36\t    # (nobody else will fix it), and every cycle it stays red is a cycle the\n37\t    # merge gate silently blocks work that was already paid for.\n38\t    \"own_pr_ci\": 90,\n39\t    # ORPHAN-723 — a third-party PR (Dependabot, a developer branch) that\n40\t    # cannot pass CI. Lowest weight in the table on purpose: ARIA has no\n41\t    # authority over these PRs until the E23 gate opens, so the row exists\n42\t    # to make the repo's PR weather VISIBLE in the nightly report, never to\n43\t    # pull work toward something ARIA may not touch.\n44\t    \"repo_pr_health\": 20,\n45\t    # SI-1 (ORPHAN-741) — ARIA's OWN pipeline has stopped moving. Kept beside its only consumer.\n426\t_MAX_QUEUE_ITEM_REMINTS = 2\n427\t\n428\t\n429\tdef _find_projected_queue_request(\n430\t    *,\n431\t    base_dir: Path,\n432\t    queue_item_id: str,\n433\t    requests: list[dict[str, Any]],\n434\t) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n435\t    marker = f'\"queue_item_id\": \"{queue_item_id}\"'\n436\t    for request in reversed(requests):\n437\t        if request.get(\"role\") != \"maintenance_utility\":\n438\t            continue\n439\t        if request.get(\"target_agent\") != \"aria-autonomy-planner\":\n440\t            continue\n441\t        prompt = str(request.get(\"suggested_prompt\") or \"\")\n442\t        if marker in prompt or queue_item_id in prompt:\n443\t            return request\n444\t    return None\n445\t\n446\t# ORPHAN-HIGH-456 — kept next to the summary they bound, so a reviewer sees\n447\t# the cap and the marker list at the point the literal is built.\n448\t_MAX_INCOMPLETE_CYCLES_IN_SUMMARY = 20\n449\t\n450\t# The cycle-level suppression/truncation markers `runtime_artifacts` sums.\n451\t# Mirrored from its `_SUPPRESSED_MARKER_KEYS` / `_TRUNCATED_MARKER_KEYS`; a\n452\t# producer that starts emitting one of these at cycle level is counted\n453\t# without further wiring, which was the stated intent of reading markers\n454\t# rather than incrementing locals.\n455\t_CYCLE_MARKER_KEYS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n456\t    \"findings_suppressed\",\n457\t    \"suppressed_count\",\n458\t    \"prompt_truncated\",\n459\t    \"truncated_count\",\n460\t)\n461\t\n462\t\n463\tdef _outer_cycle_status(projected: str) -> str:\n464\t    \"\"\"The orchestrator's verdict on a cycle: ``ok``, ``degraded`` or ``failed``.\n465\t\n466\t    ``projected`` is ``runtime_artifacts._cycle_result_status``'s word. The\n467\t    autonomy-state row and the continue/fail-closed decision both read this\n468\t    one mapping, so they cannot disagree about what a degraded night is.\n469\t    \"\"\"\n470\t    if projected in {\"ok\", \"completed\"}:\n471\t        return \"ok\"\n472\t    if projected == \"degraded\":\n473\t        return \"degraded\"\n474\t    return \"failed\"\n475\t\n476\t\n477\tdef _bounded_cycle_summary(cycle_result: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n478\t    tool_runs = cycle_result.get(\"tool_run_summary\") if isinstance(cycle_result.get(\"tool_run_summary\"), list) else []\n479\t    artifact_refs = [\n480\t        ref for ref in cycle_result.get(\"artifact_refs\", [])\n481\t        if isinstance(ref, dict)\n482\t    ] if isinstance(cycle_result.get(\"artifact_refs\"), list) else []\n483\t    failed_phases = cycle_result.get(\"failed_phases\")\n484\t    if not isinstance(failed_phases, list):\n485\t        failed_phases = [\n486\t            {\"phase\": str(item), \"status\": \"failed\"}\n487\t            for item in cycle_result.get(\"extended_phase_failures\", [])\n488\t        ] if isinstance(cycle_result.get(\"extended_phase_failures\"), list) else []\n489\t    summary = {\n490\t        \"schema_version\": 2,\n491\t        \"cycle_id\": cycle_result.get(\"cycle_id\"),\n492\t        \"status\": cycle_result.get(\"status\"),\n493\t        \"runtime_status\": cycle_result.get(\"runtime_status\", \"ok\" if cycle_result.get(\"status\") == \"completed\" else cycle_result.get(\"status\")),\n494\t        \"tool_run_summary\": tool_runs,\n495\t        \"artifact_refs\": artifact_refs,\n496\t        \"artifact_integrity\": cycle_result.get(\"artifact_integrity\"),\n497\t        \"non_ok_tools\": cycle_result.get(\"non_ok_tools\", []),\n498\t        # ARIA-HIGH-098 — the degraded tools by class, so the autonomy-state\n499\t        # row (and the doctor reading it) names the tool without resolving\n500\t        # the run artifact.\n501\t        \"degraded_tools\": cycle_result.get(\"degraded_tools\", []),\n502\t        \"failed_phases\": failed_phases,\n503\t        \"incomplete_lifecycle_count\": cycle_result.get(\"incomplete_lifecycle_count\", 0),\n504\t        # ARIA-HIGH-140 — the phase outcome ledger (ran / skipped / failed /\n505\t        # interrupted, with the reason) reaches the store: before this it\n506\t        # lived only in the cycle's in-memory state, and an operator could\n507\t        # not tell from any ledger which phase a deadline cut.\n508\t        \"phases\": cycle_result.get(\"phases\") if isinstance(cycle_result.get(\"phases\"), dict) else {},\n509\t    }\n510\t    # ORPHAN-HIGH-456 — this literal is CLOSED, so any key it does not name\n511\t    # is deleted on the way to the publisher. Two consumers were reading\n512\t    # keys that could therefore never arrive:\n513\t    #\n514\t    #   * `runtime_artifacts` raises `cycle_lifecycle_unreadable` from\n515\t    #     `cycle.get(\"cycle_lifecycle\")` — the distinction between \"zero\n516\t    #     incomplete cycles\" and \"the cycles ledger could not be read\",\n517\t    #     which is the whole point of ORPHAN-HIGH-424's fix. `cycle.py`\n518\t    #     produces the snapshot; this function dropped it one call later,\n519\t    #     so the warning was unreachable in production while its tests\n520\t    #     asserted on the raw cycle dict, a shape production never emits.\n521\t    #   * the suppression/truncation markers are summed at cycle level as\n522\t    #     well as per tool run, and no cycle-level marker could survive\n523\t    #     this literal either.\n524\t    #\n525\t    # `incomplete_cycles` is capped because it is operator-facing evidence,\n526\t    # not a data feed, and an unbounded list from a damaged ledger is how a\n527\t    # summary becomes unpublishable.\n528\t    lifecycle = cycle_result.get(\"cycle_lifecycle\")\n529\t    if isinstance(lifecycle, dict):\n530\t        incomplete = lifecycle.get(\"incomplete_cycles\")\n531\t        summary[\"cycle_lifecycle\"] = {\n532\t            \"valid\": lifecycle.get(\"valid\"),\n533\t            \"incomplete_count\": lifecycle.get(\"incomplete_count\", 0),\n534\t            \"incomplete_cycles\": (\n535\t                list(incomplete)[:_MAX_INCOMPLETE_CYCLES_IN_SUMMARY]\n536\t                if isinstance(incomplete, list)\n537\t                else []\n538\t            ),\n539\t            \"lifecycle_read_error\": lifecycle.get(\"lifecycle_read_error\"),\n540\t            \"ledger_integrity_error\": lifecycle.get(\"ledger_integrity_error\"),\n541\t        }\n542\t    for marker in _CYCLE_MARKER_KEYS:\n543\t        if marker in cycle_result:\n544\t            summary[marker] = cycle_result[marker]\n545\t    return summary\n546\t\n547\t\n548\tclass PreflightVerdict(NamedTuple):\n549\t    \"\"\"RC-5 — a preflight outcome that can carry WHY without a positional tuple.\n550\t\n551\t    ``detail`` exists because a refused policy is only actionable if the\n552\t    operator is told which key in which file is wrong. The reason code is for\n553\t    the state machine; the detail is for the human, carried verbatim from the\n554\t    GovernanceError rather than re-worded, so the message the operator reads is\n555\t    the message the code raised.\n556\t    \"\"\"\n557\t\n558\t    status: str\n559\t    reason: str | None = None"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"36148bec-0d82-47e8-b0dd-b970b025de87","uuid":"3e15a7d1-1077-4efb-911c-2ec826d89616","timestamp":"2026-09-20T12:33:33.229Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-autonomy-planner-310a6c39c90e/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/autonomy_orchestrator.py","content":"\n\n\n# Y3 (ORPHAN-703) — successor budget for dead projected-queue envelopes,\n# mirroring DEFAULT_MAX_REQUEUES: two lineage steps then an exhausted\n# disclosure. Kept beside its only consumer.\n_MAX_QUEUE_ITEM_REMINTS = 2\n\n\ndef _find_projected_queue_request(\n    *,\n    base_dir: Path,\n    queue_item_id: str,\n    requests: list[dict[str, Any]],\n) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n    marker = f'\"queue_item_id\": \"{queue_item_id}\"'\n    for request in reversed(requests):\n        if request.get(\"role\") != \"maintenance_utility\":\n            continue\n        if request.get(\"target_agent\") != \"aria-autonomy-planner\":\n            continue\n        prompt = str(request.get(\"suggested_prompt\") or \"\")\n        if marker in prompt or queue_item_id in prompt:\n            return request\n    return None\n\n# ORPHAN-HIGH-456 — kept next to the summary they bound, so a reviewer sees\n# the cap and the marker list at the point the literal is built.\n_MAX_INCOMPLETE_CYCLES_IN_SUMMARY = 20\n\n# The cycle-level suppression/truncation markers `runtime_artifacts` sums.\n# Mirrored from its `_SUPPRESSED_MARKER_KEYS` / `_TRUNCATED_MARKER_KEYS`; a\n# producer that starts emitting one of these at cycle level is counted\n# without further wiring, which was the stated intent of reading markers\n# rather than incrementing locals.\n_CYCLE_MARKER_KEYS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"findings_suppressed\",\n    \"suppressed_count\",\n    \"prompt_truncated\",\n    \"truncated_count\",\n)\n\n\ndef _outer_cycle_status(projected: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"The orchestrator's verdict on a cycle: ``ok``, ``degraded`` or ``failed``.\n\n    ``projected`` is ``runtime_artifacts._cycle_result_status``'s word. The\n    autonomy-state row and the continue/fail-closed decision both read this\n    one mapping, so they cannot disagree about what a degraded night is.\n    \"\"\"\n    if projected in {\"ok\", \"completed\"}:\n        return \"ok\"\n    if projected == \"degraded\":\n        return \"degraded\"\n    return \"failed\"\n\n\ndef _bounded_cycle_summary(cycle_result: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    tool_runs = cycle_result.get(\"tool_run_summary\") if isinstance(cycle_result.get(\"tool_run_summary\"), list) else []\n    artifact_refs = [\n        ref for ref in cycle_result.get(\"artifact_refs\", [])\n        if isinstance(ref, dict)\n    ] if isinstance(cycle_result.get(\"artifact_refs\"), list) else []\n    failed_phases = cycle_result.get(\"failed_phases\")\n    if not isinstance(failed_phases, list):\n        failed_phases = [\n            {\"phase\": str(item), \"status\": \"failed\"}\n            for item in cycle_result.get(\"extended_phase_failures\", [])\n        ] if isinstance(cycle_result.get(\"extended_phase_failures\"), list) else []\n    summary = {\n        \"schema_version\": 2,\n        \"cycle_id\": cycle_result.get(\"cycle_id\"),\n        \"status\": cycle_result.get(\"status\"),\n        \"runtime_status\": cycle_result.get(\"runtime_status\", \"ok\" if cycle_result.get(\"status\") == \"completed\" else cycle_result.get(\"status\")),\n        \"tool_run_summary\": tool_runs,\n        \"artifact_refs\": artifact_refs,\n        \"artifact_integrity\": cycle_result.get(\"artifact_integrity\"),\n        \"non_ok_tools\": cycle_result.get(\"non_ok_tools\", []),\n        # ARIA-HIGH-098 — the degraded tools by class, so the autonomy-state\n        # row (and the doctor reading it) names the tool without resolving\n        # the run artifact.\n        \"degraded_tools\": cycle_result.get(\"degraded_tools\", []),\n        \"failed_phases\": failed_phases,\n        \"incomplete_lifecycle_count\": cycle_result.get(\"incomplete_lifecycle_count\", 0),\n        # ARIA-HIGH-140 — the phase outcome ledger (ran / skipped / failed /\n        # interrupted, with the reason) reaches the store: before this it\n        # lived only in the cycle's in-memory state, and an operator could\n        # not tell from any ledger which phase a deadline cut.\n        \"phases\": cycle_result.get(\"phases\") if isinstance(cycle_result.get(\"phases\"), dict) else {},\n    }\n    # ORPHAN-HIGH-456 — this literal is CLOSED, so any key it does not name\n    # is deleted on the way to the publisher. Two consumers were reading\n    # keys that could therefore never arrive:\n    #\n    #   * `runtime_artifacts` raises `cycle_lifecycle_unreadable` from\n    #     `cycle.get(\"cycle_lifecycle\")` — the distinction between \"zero\n    #     incomplete cycles\" and \"the cycles ledger could not be read\",\n    #     which is the whole point of ORPHAN-HIGH-424's fix. `cycle.py`\n    #     produces the snapshot; this function dropped it one call later,\n    #     so the warning was unreachable in production while its tests\n    #     asserted on the raw cycle dict, a shape production never emits.\n    #   * the suppression/truncation markers are summed at cycle level as\n    #     well as per tool run, and no cycle-level marker could survive\n    #     this literal either.\n    #\n    # `incomplete_cycles` is capped because it is operator-facing evidence,\n    # not a data feed, and an unbounded list from a damaged ledger is how a\n    # summary becomes unpublishable.\n    lifecycle = cycle_result.get(\"cycle_lifecycle\")\n    if isinstance(lifecycle, dict):\n        incomplete = lifecycle.get(\"incomplete_cycles\")\n        summary[\"cycle_lifecycle\"] = {\n            \"valid\": lifecycle.get(\"valid\"),\n            \"incomplete_count\": lifecycle.get(\"incomplete_count\", 0),\n            \"incomplete_cycles\": (\n                list(incomplete)[:_MAX_INCOMPLETE_CYCLES_IN_SUMMARY]\n                if isinstance(incomplete, list)\n                else []\n            ),\n            \"lifecycle_read_error\": lifecycle.get(\"lifecycle_read_error\"),\n            \"ledger_integrity_error\": lifecycle.get(\"ledger_integrity_error\"),\n        }\n    for marker in _CYCLE_MARKER_KEYS:\n        if marker in cycle_result:\n            summary[marker] = cycle_result[marker]\n    return summary\n\n\nclass PreflightVerdict(NamedTuple):\n    \"\"\"RC-5 — a preflight outcome that can carry WHY without a positional tuple.\n\n    ``detail`` exists because a refused policy is only actionable if the\n    operator is told which key in which file is wrong. The reason code is for\n    the state machine; the detail is for the human, carried verbatim from the\n    GovernanceError rather than re-worded, so the message the operator reads is\n    the message the code raised.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    status: str\n    reason: str | None = None","numLines":140,"startLine":420,"totalLines":2833}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01ScneCuCLWbPjJsMRmTYxJu","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"The self-change contract: what a `propose_self_change` mission asks an agent, and how the answer reaches the kernel.\n2\t\n3\tWHY: `open_self_improvement_missions` (Plan 032 Faz 032i) minted missions\n4\twhose `next_action == \"propose_self_change\"`, the orchestrator's mission\n5\tbranch forwarded that string as free prompt text under the generic\n6\t`queue_item_projected` contract, and NOTHING mapped an accepted answer onto\n7\t`propose_self_change` — so `authority_surface_violations` and the\n8\tHUMAN_REQUIRED adjudication could only ever fire from the CLI. Confirmed\n9\tlive 2026-09-12 (the ORPHAN-694 class: mechanism present, caller absent).\n10\t\n11\tWHAT: one contract, three consumers.\n12\t* `self_change_must_satisfy()` / `build_self_change_prompt()` — what the\n13\t  mission branch mints (`aria/self-change-request/v1`): the agent must answer\n14\t  with `details.evidence_paths`, `details.problem`, `details.proposed_change`.\n15\t* `validate_self_change_response()` — the SHAPE check, used by the executor's\n16\t  pre-submit gate (release + retry, harness-class) and by the bridge, so the\n17\t  two can never disagree about what a well-formed answer is (the Y5 lesson).\n18\t* `record_self_change_result()` — the accepted-result bridge: reads the\n19\t  structured fields off the envelope, calls `propose_self_change` (which is\n20\t  where the AUTHORITY boundary is judged — authority surfaces AND the\n21\t  mission's state, pointer and open adjudication — and the HUMAN_REQUIRED\n22\t  opens), then parks the mission in HUMAN_REQUIRED, a WAITING state\n23\t  `mission_scheduler.select_next_mission` skips. A refusal raises before the\n24\t  park, so a refused answer leaves the mission exactly as it was.\n25\t\n26\tThe bridge dispatches on the CONTRACT the request declared (its must_satisfy\n27\tids, part of the request identity), never on the agent's prose; the mission\n28\tidentity comes from the request's `pressure_event_id` (D1: identity from what\n29\twas asked, never from what the agent volunteers).\n30\t\"\"\"\n31\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n32\t\n33\tfrom pathlib import Path\n34\tfrom typing import Any\n35\t\n36\tfrom .self_improvement import (\n37\t    AUTHORITY_SURFACES,\n38\t    DEFAULT_VALIDATION_COMMAND,\n39\t    SELF_CHANGE_ALLOWED_PREFIXES,\n40\t    SELF_CHANGE_NEXT_ACTION,\n41\t)\n42\tfrom .tool_registry import GovernanceError, ensure_tools_dir\n43\t\n44\tSELF_CHANGE_REQUEST_SCHEMA = \"aria/self-change-request/v1\"\n45\t# The same lane the queue projection uses: the planner is read-only and\n46\t# kernel-envelope only, which is exactly the shape a PROPOSAL needs — the\n47\t# change itself is applied by nobody until a person adjudicates.\n48\tSELF_CHANGE_TARGET_AGENT = \"aria-autonomy-planner\"\n49\tSELF_CHANGE_ROLE = \"maintenance_utility\"\n50\t# The structured fields `propose_self_change` takes, in the order the\n51\t# contract names them; `details.<field>` on the response envelope.\n52\tSELF_CHANGE_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\"evidence_paths\", \"problem\", \"proposed_change\")\n53\tSELF_CHANGE_MUST_SATISFY: tuple[dict[str, str], ...] = (\n54\t    {\"id\": \"self_change_evidence_paths\",\n55\t     \"description\": \"details.evidence_paths is a non-empty list of repo-relative file paths that evidence the defect, \"\n56\t                  \"every one inside ARIA's own scope (allowed_prefixes) and none on an authority surface \"\n57\t                  \"(authority_surfaces); each path was read at the request's target SHA\"},\n58\t    {\"id\": \"self_change_problem\",\n59\t     \"description\": \"details.problem states the defect the mission's signal evidences, grounded in evidence_paths\"},\n60\t    {\"id\": \"self_change_proposed_change\",\n61\t     \"description\": \"details.proposed_change states the concrete change to ARIA's own code and how validation_command proves it\"},\n62\t)\n63\tSELF_CHANGE_CONTRACT_IDS: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(item[\"id\"] for item in SELF_CHANGE_MUST_SATISFY)\n64\t# The executor's release reason for a malformed answer — harness-class (the\n65\t# shape says nothing about the mission; re-asking usually succeeds), listed\n66\t# in agent_invocations.HARNESS_FAULT_RELEASE_REASONS.\n67\tSELF_CHANGE_CONTRACT_RELEASE_REASON = \"self_change_contract_violation\"\n68\tSELF_CHANGE_STEP_PREFIX = \"self_change:\"\n69\tSELF_CHANGE_MISSION_REASON = \"self_change_adjudication\"\n70\t\n71\t\n72\tdef self_change_must_satisfy() -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n73\t    return [dict(item) for item in SELF_CHANGE_MUST_SATISFY]\n74\t\n75\t\n76\tdef self_change_allowed_scope() -> list[str]:\n77\t    \"\"\"`SELF_CHANGE_ALLOWED_PREFIXES` as evidence-validator globs (`dir/**`, `stem*`).\"\"\"\n78\t    return [prefix + \"**\" if prefix.endswith(\"/\") else prefix + \"*\" for prefix in SELF_CHANGE_ALLOWED_PREFIXES]\n79\t\n80\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"36148bec-0d82-47e8-b0dd-b970b025de87","uuid":"5d96a87f-cdf1-4017-b4b1-9eb5b0c45d11","timestamp":"2026-09-20T12:33:34.604Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-autonomy-planner-310a6c39c90e/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/self_change_bridge.py","content":"\"\"\"The self-change contract: what a `propose_self_change` mission asks an agent, and how the answer reaches the kernel.\n\nWHY: `open_self_improvement_missions` (Plan 032 Faz 032i) minted missions\nwhose `next_action == \"propose_self_change\"`, the orchestrator's mission\nbranch forwarded that string as free prompt text under the generic\n`queue_item_projected` contract, and NOTHING mapped an accepted answer onto\n`propose_self_change` — so `authority_surface_violations` and the\nHUMAN_REQUIRED adjudication could only ever fire from the CLI. A refusal raises before the\n  park, so a refused answer leaves the mission exactly as it was.\n\nThe bridge dispatches on the CONTRACT the request declared (its must_satisfy\nids, part of the request identity), never on the agent's prose; `details.<field>` on the response envelope.\nSELF_CHANGE_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\"evidence_paths\", \"problem\", \"proposed_change\")\nSELF_CHANGE_MUST_SATISFY: tuple[dict[str, str], ...] = (\n    {\"id\": \"self_change_evidence_paths\",\n     \"description\": \"details.evidence_paths is a non-empty list of repo-relative file paths that evidence the defect, \"\n                  \"every one inside ARIA's own scope (allowed_prefixes) and none on an authority surface \"\n                  \"(authority_surfaces); re-asking usually succeeds), listed\n# in agent_invocations.HARNESS_FAULT_RELEASE_REASONS.\nSELF_CHANGE_CONTRACT_RELEASE_REASON = \"self_change_contract_violation\"\nSELF_CHANGE_STEP_PREFIX = \"self_change:\"\nSELF_CHANGE_MISSION_REASON = \"self_change_adjudication\"\n\n\ndef self_change_must_satisfy() -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    return [dict(item) for item in SELF_CHANGE_MUST_SATISFY]\n\n\ndef self_change_allowed_scope() -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"`SELF_CHANGE_ALLOWED_PREFIXES` as evidence-validator globs (`dir/**`, `stem*`).\"\"\"\n    return [prefix + \"**\" if prefix.endswith(\"/\") else prefix + \"*\" for prefix in SELF_CHANGE_ALLOWED_PREFIXES]\n\n","numLines":80,"startLine":1,"totalLines":197}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KxmtPFDJ2CwMnMQiBZ1qtL","type":"tool_result","content":"580\t    # compared against an anchor the submitter did not cite.\n581\t    probe_session = probe_session if probe_session is not None else GitProbeSession()\n582\t\n583\t    response_refs = response.get(\"evidence_refs\") or []\n584\t    if not isinstance(response_refs, list):\n585\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_refs_not_list\"})\n586\t        response_refs = []\n587\t    for ref in response_refs:\n588\t        if not isinstance(ref, str) or not ref.strip():\n589\t            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_ref_not_string\"})\n590\t            continue\n591\t        _check_agent_ref(\n592\t            ref, root=root, errors=errors, checked=checked,\n593\t            allow_kernel_artifacts=allow_kernel_artifacts,\n594\t        )\n595\t        if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(ref):\n596\t            continue  # Z2 — a ledger pointer has no repo classification\n597\t        if allow_kernel_artifacts and _kernel_artifact_verdict(ref, root)[0]:\n598\t            # Verified by content hash against the results ledger — the\n599\t            # repo classification below cannot describe it (the artifact\n600\t            # is state-store-owned and was never meant to be committed).\n601\t            continue\n602\t        envelope = classify_evidence_ref(\n603\t            ref,\n604\t            workspace_root=root,\n605\t            source_hint=\"agent_output\",\n606\t            target_sha=_request_target_sha(request),\n607\t            probe_session=probe_session,\n608\t        )\n609\t        evidence_envelopes.append(envelope.to_dict())\n610\t        try:\n611\t            EvidencePolicy.require_repo_verified(envelope)\n612\t        except GovernanceError as exc:\n613\t            errors.append({\n614\t                \"code\": _unverified_evidence_code(\"agent\", envelope.trust_grade),\n615\t                \"ref\": ref,\n616\t                \"reason\": str(exc),\n617\t            })\n618\t\n619\t    # Plan 024 §B-2 — satisfaction_matrix non-empty enforcement.\n620\t    # Pre-fix an agent response with `satisfaction_matrix: []` passed\n621\t    # consensus because the matrix-iteration loop simply never ran.\n622\t    # Combined with the empty-allowed_scope skip (line below), a judge\n623\t    # could rubber-stamp a request with zero criteria checked. Writing raw JSONL to output_path means\n2234\t    # kernel submit-result reads runtime telemetry instead of the envelope,\n2235\t    # which fails:\n2236\t    #   response_schema: missing required fields:\n2237\t    #     ['$schema', 'request_id', 'claim_id', 'agent_id', 'role',\n2238\t    #      'status', 'satisfaction_matrix']\n2239\t    #\n2240\t    # HOW: parse JSONL, extract the final message, find the embedded\n2241\t    # envelope JSON, INJECT mandatory identity fields ($schema,\n2242\t    # request_id, claim_id, agent_id, role, status) from the known\n2243\t    # ci_executor context (these aren't the agent's job — the agent\n2244\t    # only knows its plan content), synthesize a satisfaction_matrix\n2245\t    # from must_satisfy with verdict=satisfied when the agent omitted\n2246\t    # it, then write the corrected envelope to output_path.\n2247\t    #\n2248\t    # Tier hierarchy: Tier-2 (Make it automatic) — the envelope shape\n2249\t    # is now produced correctly by default; agents don't have to know\n2250\t    # internal kernel identity fields.\n2251\t    if completed.stdout:\n2252\t        envelope = _build_envelope_from_claude_output(\n2253\t            raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n2254\t            request_id=request_id,\n2255\t            claim_id=claim_id or \"\",\n2256\t            agent_id=agent_id or \"\",\n2257\t            role=role,\n2258\t            subagent_type=subagent_type,\n2259\t            must_satisfy=must_satisfy or [],\n2260\t            dispatch_model=agent_profile.model,\n2261\t        )\n2262\t        envelope[\"details\"][\"agent_contract_hash\"] = agent_contract.contract_hash\n2263\t        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n2264\t        _write_sanitized_envelope(output_path, envelope)\n2265\t        resolved_transcript_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n2266\t        resolved_transcript_path.write_text(completed.stdout, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n2267\t        # Plan ARIA-V3.1-D3 — per-LLM-call cost attribution. Gated on\n2268\t        # _MOCK_MODE_AT_ENTRY frozen sentinel (V3.1-D2) so a mid-run\n2269\t        # CLAUDE_CLI_MOCK flip cannot rewrite mock-mode classification\n2270\t        # between mint + record sites. request_envelope + tools_dir\n2271\t        # MUST both be supplied for the record to fire — None defaults\n2272\t        # preserve V8 backward-compat for callers that haven't migrated.\n2273\t        if (\n2274\t            completed.returncode == 0\n2275\t            and _MOCK_MODE_AT_ENTRY is False\n2276\t            and request_envelope is not None\n2277\t            and tools_dir is not None\n2278\t        ):\n2279\t            _record_claude_cli_usage(\n2280\t                raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n2281\t                request_envelope=request_envelope,\n2282\t                tools_dir=tools_dir,\n2283\t                role=role,\n2284\t                request_id=request_id,\n2285\t                signer_key_fp=signer_key_fp,\n2286\t            )\n2287\t    _emit_dispatch_summary(\n2288\t        outcome=\"succeeded\" if completed.returncode == 0 else \"failed\",\n2289\t        failure=classify_dispatch_failure(result=completed, phase=\"runtime\"),\n2290\t        exit_code=completed.returncode,\n2291\t    )\n2292\t    if completed.returncode != 0 and completed.stderr:\n2293\t        # Plan ARIA-V7 §2g v2 + V7.10 envelope-extraction fix.\n    #\n    # WHY: claude -p stream-json emits JSONL events\n    # ({\"result\": \"...\", \"total_cost_usd\": ..., ...}) where the agent's final message may contain the\n    # actual aria/agent-response/v1 envelope as fenced ```json``` block\n    # or as final JSON block. Writing raw JSONL to output_path means\n    # kernel submit-result reads runtime telemetry instead of the envelope,\n    # which fails:\n    #   response_schema: missing required fields:\n    #     ['$schema', 'request_id', 'claim_id', 'agent_id', 'role',\n    #      'status', 'satisfaction_matrix']\n    #\n    # HOW: parse JSONL, extract the final message, find the embedded\n    # envelope JSON, INJECT mandatory identity fields ($schema,\n    # request_id, claim_id, agent_id, role, status) from the known\n    # ci_executor context (these aren't the agent's job — the agent\n    # only knows its plan content), synthesize a satisfaction_matrix\n    # from must_satisfy with verdict=satisfied when the agent omitted\n    # it, then write the corrected envelope to output_path.\n    #\n    # Tier hierarchy: Tier-2 (Make it automatic) — the envelope shape\n    # is now produced correctly by default; agents don't have to know\n    # internal kernel identity fields.\n    if completed.stdout:\n        envelope = _build_envelope_from_claude_output(\n            raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n            request_id=request_id,\n            claim_id=claim_id or \"\",\n            agent_id=agent_id or \"\",\n            role=role,\n            subagent_type=subagent_type,\n            must_satisfy=must_satisfy or [],\n            dispatch_model=agent_profile.model,\n        )\n        envelope[\"details\"][\"agent_contract_hash\"] = agent_contract.contract_hash\n        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        _write_sanitized_envelope(output_path, envelope)\n        resolved_transcript_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        resolved_transcript_path.write_text(completed.stdout, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        # Plan ARIA-V3.1-D3 — per-LLM-call cost attribution. Gated on\n        # _MOCK_MODE_AT_ENTRY frozen sentinel (V3.1-D2) so a mid-run\n        # CLAUDE_CLI_MOCK flip cannot rewrite mock-mode classification\n        # between mint + record sites. request_envelope + tools_dir\n        # MUST both be supplied for the record to fire — None defaults\n        # preserve V8 backward-compat for callers that haven't migrated.\n        if (\n            completed.returncode == 0\n            and _MOCK_MODE_AT_ENTRY is False\n            and request_envelope is not None\n            and tools_dir is not None\n        ):\n            _record_claude_cli_usage(\n                raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n                request_envelope=request_envelope,\n                tools_dir=tools_dir,\n                role=role,\n                request_id=request_id,\n                signer_key_fp=signer_key_fp,\n            )\n    _emit_dispatch_summary(\n        outcome=\"succeeded\" if completed.returncode == 0 else \"failed\",\n        failure=classify_dispatch_failure(result=completed, phase=\"runtime\"),\n        exit_code=completed.returncode,\n    )\n    if completed.returncode != 0 and completed.stderr:\n        # The child's own last words, bounded and lease-redacted: a non-zero\n        # exit with no cause is the operator reading four ledgers to learn\n        # that the limiter could not reach a bus.\n        sys.stderr.write(\"claude_stderr_tail: \" + _redact_lease_in_message(completed.stderr[-2000:], None) + \"\\n\")\n    return completed.returncode\n\n\ndef _record_claude_cli_usage(\n    *,\n    raw_stdout: str,\n    request_envelope: dict[str, Any],\n    tools_dir: Path,","numLines":80,"startLine":2225,"totalLines":5265}}}
